### Image prompt
````text
Copy the FIRST attached reference photo EXACTLY as a photograph, changing only the room finishes and people described below: a different two-storey American Hamptons shingle-style entrance hall, the same camera position standing on the hall floor a few steps back and tilted up, the same eye level, the same distance, the same vertical 9:16 framing, the same very tall slim Christmas tree standing in the middle of the frame and rising the full height of the picture past the staircase, a pale-oak staircase with slim black wrought-iron balusters climbing from the lower right of the frame up to the upper left behind the tree, the same second run of railing higher up, a heavy white-painted newel post in the lower right corner, white-painted shiplap walls and white crown moulding, the same pale ceiling across the top, the same open doorway and ceiling fan visible at the left, the same tall painted wooden nutcracker standing at the far left, a wide pale-oak wood floor. Same composition, same perspective, same scale of the tree. Change only the room finishes and the people; retain every object's position.

THE PEOPLE - REMOVE AND REPLACE: the mother who is up on the staircase in the FIRST reference is replaced by a different MOTHER in exactly her position, at exactly her height on the stairs, at exactly her distance from the camera and exactly her size in the frame - she is up on the upper run of the staircase behind the slim black wrought-iron railing, leaning far out over that railing toward the Christmas tree, one hand gripping the rail, her other arm stretched out across the gap toward the top of the tree, holding a Christmas tree topper and reaching to set it on the highest tip. She is a mother about 31 with warm medium-tan skin and long dark wavy hair in a low ponytail. She is on the STAIRS, over the railing - not on the floor. The man standing at the lower left of the reference is gone. Below her, standing together on the hall FLOOR at the bottom of the frame over to the LEFT side of the frame, on the opposite side of the picture from the staircase and the newel post, well clear of the tree and NOT in front of it - the floor directly in front of the tree's base is left completely empty - with their backs to the camera, small in the frame because they are far below the top of the tree, two children - a boy about 7 with deep brown skin and short black coily hair and a girl about 6 with fair skin, rosy cheeks and a short dark-brown bob with a straight fringe - are seen from behind, standing on the floor with their heads tipped back looking up at their mother and at the top of the tall tree, the boy holding the little girl's hand. The tree still towers over all of them and still fills the height of the frame exactly as it does in the reference.

THEIR CLOTHES: all three wear matching American-flag Christmas pyjamas - pyjama tops with bold red and white horizontal stripes and a navy blue yoke across the shoulders covered in small white stars, and matching red and white striped pyjama bottoms, soft brushed cotton, slightly loose the way real pyjamas fit - with thick cream wool socks.

ADD ONE THING: a real fabric United States flag hangs flat against the white shiplap wall in the background at the left, behind the family, clearly readable stars and stripes, hanging straight and still. Keep it modest - about the size of a large poster, noticeably smaller than the tree - so it does not dominate the wall, does not cover the doorway or the staircase, and does not reach up into the empty band of ceiling at the top of the frame.

The tree stays exactly as it is in the reference: the same very tall slim green artificial tree, the same dense old-fashioned dressing of mismatched multicoloured glass baubles, candy canes, snowmen, tinsel garland and warm white string lights, and the same wrapped presents piled at its base. THE VERY TOP OF THE TREE IS BARE - there is no star and no topper sitting on it, because the mother is in the act of reaching out from the stairs to place one there; the gold star topper is in her outstretched hand, held just clear of the highest tip and not yet fixed to it.

FRAMING: leave a clean band of plain empty ceiling across the very top of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame - free of the tree top, of the star, of the mother's outstretched arm, of the railing and of any other object, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the tree to make that room.

COLOUR: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a warm room. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The walls read as clean white-painted shiplap, the staircase and floor as natural pale oak, the flag keeps true red, white and navy.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean and evenly lit daytime interior, plenty of light on the walls and on the family, no dark corners, no heavy shadows. Shot on an iPhone and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere, crisp detail in the ornaments, the tinsel and the knit of the pyjamas, realistic skin and fabric texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

No captions, no watermarks, no lettering, no user-interface elements and no social-media overlays anywhere in the image.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact camera, framing, person positions, staircase geometry, tree scale and iPhone photograph look. @Image 2 supplies only the Hamptons shingle-style house's white shiplap and pale-oak material look and the new children's hair and appearance. Do not import its composition, poses, projected imagery, darkness or lighting. This hook is a brightly and evenly lit daytime hall with plain white shiplap walls. Keep the American-flag pyjamas from @Image 1. The children remain on the LEFT, away from the staircase and clear of the tree base. The mother remains leaning over the railing at the same scale and position.
````

````text
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, filmed by someone standing on the hallway floor holding the phone up — real held-phone look with natural handheld sway, no cuts, no zoom, no lens push-in, framing locked exactly as in the start frame for the whole clip.

Start frame: a woman in American-flag pajamas stands on the staircase behind the pale-oak railing, leaning out over it with one arm stretched up to the gold star on top of the tall decorated Christmas tree beside the stairs. Two small children in matching American-flag pajamas stand on the floor at the lower left of the frame with their backs to the camera, watching her. Wrapped presents at the foot of the tree.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0–1.0s — Both hands now on the gold star, she works it down onto the top of the tree, stretching further and further out over the railing, more and more of her weight going onto the tree itself.
1.0–1.6s — The tree tips. It leans away from the stairs toward the open floor and keeps going, and she loses her balance and comes over the railing with it, still holding on. At the same instant the two children take three quick steps further to the left, well out of the way, and stop there — the tree never comes near them.
1.6–2.4s — The whole tree swings down diagonally across the frame and crashes flat onto the wooden floor, branches flattening, the gold star snapping off the top and skittering away on its own.
2.4–3.2s — She lands on the floor face-down across the fallen tree, and the impact bursts a wave of ornaments off the branches — dozens of red, gold, pink and white baubles bouncing out low across the wooden floor and rolling toward the camera, spreading right across the bottom of the frame.
3.2–5.0s — The last ornaments wobble to a stop on the floor. She lies still across the fallen tree for a beat, then lifts her head, blows the hair out of her face and starts laughing. The two children stay where they moved to on the left, staring at the mess. End frame: the tree flat on the floor, ornaments scattered everywhere, her looking up laughing.

AUDIO: her real voice, lip-synced, matching her appearance — a short yelp as she goes over, then breathless laughing at the end; the dry rush of the tree branches going over; the heavy thud of the tree hitting the wooden floor; dozens of glass and plastic ornaments clattering, bouncing and rolling on hard wood; one small surprised squeal from a child. (no BGM). No subtitles.

REALISM — HARD RULES: she keeps exactly the same face, hair and American-flag pajamas as the start frame the whole clip, and the two children keep exactly the same clothes and size. The hallway stays pixel-faithful to the start frame — same staircase, same pale-oak railing and white-painted newel post, same American flag on the wall, same wooden floor, same daylight. The children are never touched by the tree and are never underneath it. Real weight and gravity — the tree topples on its own mass and stops on the floor, she drops straight down, nobody is thrown or launched. Real phone-camera look, slight sensor noise, mild motion blur on the fall, no cinematic bokeh, no slow motion, no text overlays, no watermark. No subtitles, no captions and no on-screen words of any kind anywhere in the picture.

POSITIVE LOCKS: everything plays at real-time speed. One continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s. The camera never moves from where it is in the start frame.
````

### Image prompt
````text
Copy the FIRST attached reference photo EXACTLY: same camera position, same top-down-ish angle, same distance, same vertical 9:16 framing, same table surface at the same height and same angle, same two hands resting on the box in the same position and same grip, same arms entering frame the same way. Keep OUR product box exactly as it appears in the FIRST reference and in the SECOND attached reference: a tall upright rectangular tuck-top carton, square footprint, roughly 15 x 15 x 24 cm, forest green body, dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, cream serif headline CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed render of a smooth white dome-topped cylindrical projector on a separate white four-legged splayed stand base, slim red remote printed beside it, gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. Keep the box at the same position, scale and angle it occupies in the FIRST reference. Change only three things.

FIRST - THE HANDS. A woman's hands, about 27, with light caramel skin and slender fingers, slender fingers, narrow wrists, smooth bare skin, short clean natural nails with no polish, no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch - the cuffs of a dusty-blue chunky cable-knit wool sweater pushed back at both wrists. Same position on the box, same grip, same arms entering the frame the same way, same scale.

SECOND - THE TABLE. The surface under the box is now a black-stained ash dining table with open visible grain, a satin-matte finish and a crisp square edge. Same height, same angle, same amount of the surface visible as in the FIRST reference.

THIRD - THE BACKGROUND. Behind and above the table, a festive Art Deco dining room with deep burgundy lacquered walls, a fluted brass-framed round mirror, a black marble fireplace with gold knitted stockings, a curved cream sideboard and a Christmas tree dressed with champagne-gold baubles, black velvet bows and warm white fairy lights. Every background detail stays in focus; the box remains dominant through its scale and placement.

Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few dried orange slices, two or three small champagne-gold and deep red glass baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own carton.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and carton artwork only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. All parts of this photograph, including the background, are in crisp natural iPhone deep focus, no portrait mode, no bokeh, no blur.
````

````text
STARTING-FRAME AND PRINT LOCK - use the attached starting image exactly at frame 0, including the front-facing THEXMASGLOW carton. Keep the dark-red brand band, cream CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR lettering, printed white projector and red remote, gold star/tree and gold village artwork visible and unchanged on that same rigid front panel throughout. The box stays upright and never rotates away. The left hand braces its lower side without covering the main artwork. Opening only bends the top flaps; it never erases the front printing.

AUDIO RULE - HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: this clip has NO MUSIC OF ANY KIND. No background song, no soundtrack, no score, no melody, no instruments, no singing, no humming, no jingle, no Christmas song, no festive music bed, no ambient musical pad, no strings, no piano, no bells used as music. The audio track contains ONLY real natural sound recorded in that room. No video reference is attached. If any music appears anywhere in the 4 seconds the clip is wrong.

HAND ROLES - SECOND MOST IMPORTANT: THE RIGHT HAND opens the lid and then grips and lifts the projector alone. THE LEFT HAND stays braced against the lower left side of THE BOX from first frame to last, fingers resting below the main printed artwork. The left hand never lifts, follows the projector, gestures, reaches into the box or leaves the carton. It steadies the box on the table while the right hand and product rise. Exactly two hands with separate jobs: right hand carries the product, left hand anchors the carton.

THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the wall shown in the starting frame and the whole barrel-vaulted ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ALPINE CHRISTMAS MOUNTAIN VILLAGE: wooden chalets with glowing windows climb a snowy mountainside, a little red cable car glides above them on a lit wire, families on wooden sledges slide down lantern-lit slopes, a small stone church with a lit steeple stands among them, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the chalet roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc through a sky of big glowing six-point snowflakes, twinkling golden stars and softly glowing star-shaped lanterns, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among tiny golden lights. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and alpine evergreen dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MAIN SUBJECT. The Christmas picture on the walls and ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, not a still illustration; only the projected picture is animated, while the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in every frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. The architecture, the chalets, the church and the mountains stay put; everything else drawn in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is not parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc, starting where it sits in the start image and flying on across the barrel-vaulted ceiling to the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place on the ceiling every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. While it travels the reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE: tall soft white smoke plumes stream continuously out of EVERY chalet chimney on the mountainside, rising in long wavy columns up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading as fresh puffs push up from the chimney below. Several plumes are clearly rising at once, all the time.
- SKY: the big glowing six-point snowflakes spin on their centres and drift across the ceiling; the star-shaped lanterns sway and pulse brighter and dimmer; stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across.
- MOUNTAIN LIFE: the little red cable car glides steadily along its lit wire; families on wooden sledges slide down the lantern-lit slopes; chalet windows flicker.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall, flat on the wall and ceiling surfaces.
- LIGHTS: bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: the sleigh team is already flying across the vaulted ceiling, reindeer galloping; smoke rises from the chalet chimneys on the wall behind the bed.
1-2s: the whole team has travelled a quarter of the ceiling to a new place; the cable car glides; snow drifts down the wall.
2-3s: the sleigh flies on across the vault; big snowflakes spin; new smoke puffs climb above the roofs; sledges slide down the slopes.
3-4s: the sleigh is still travelling toward the far side of the ceiling; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: everything still moving at the same pace in the last frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, shiplap and window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, furniture or children.

0.0-2.5s from the start framing the camera tilts UP very slowly and gently, by only about eight degrees, so the flying sleigh drifts toward the centre of the frame while THE BOY stays in the lower part of the frame. Every part of the barrel-vaulted ceiling that comes into view is already fully covered with the same moving projection, edge to edge, exactly like the start image - no bare white plaster, no unlit strip, no unprojected corner in any frame.
2.5-5.0s the camera tilts very slowly back DOWN and pulls back a little, the frame opening slightly wider until the bed and THE BOY fill the lower half. Both moves are slow and smooth like a calm parent filming, never quick.
THE BOY JUMPS AT REAL SPEED for all five seconds: quick springy bounces, about one full jump every 1.2 seconds. Each jump: knees bend, a fast push-off, a brief moment in the air, a fast drop under normal gravity, the mattress compressing and springing back; arms swinging, laughing. DO NOT INCLUDE: blank or partly blank walls or ceiling at the start; a projection appearing, switching on, fading in, spreading, growing or revealing itself during the shot; an opening before-and-after transformation; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; a ribbon that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a cable car that does not travel, sledges that do not slide, or star lanterns that do not sway; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; an un-animated area of the hipped ceiling or an un-animated ceiling detail in any frame; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the existing illustrated cable car glides along its wire and the families sled down the slopes on the wall; chalet windows flicker; the star lanterns sway; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The four sloping ceiling planes, their hip lines and the central ridge remain stationary. Drawings bend at every hip line, fold around the stone chimney breast corners and carry across the wooden window frames, French doors and board-and-batten panelling, with correct surface perspective and occlusion. All physical architecture stays stationary.

CURRENT BRIEF — STRONG CONTINUOUS MOTION: The existing little red cable car glides steadily along its lit wire above the illustrated chalets on the walls; families on wooden sledges slide down the lantern-lit slopes; the chalet windows flicker and the church steeple light glows. Preserve the artwork already visible. Every illustrated chimney emits fresh curling smoke continuously, moving at least one chimney height per second; the sleigh crosses a substantial part of the ceiling with galloping reindeer. Existing ribbon light fields carry clearly travelling aurora-like waves along their length, with brightness crests moving rather than blinking in place. The existing star-shaped lanterns on the ceiling are ALWAYS in motion: they sway gently and glow brighter and dimmer while the big six-point snowflakes turn and drift around them, continuously from the first frame to the last, never a still painted sky. The chalets, the church and the mountains stay anchored while characters, vehicles, smoke and light waves travel independently. The only physical motion in the room is the specified children and camera; all Christmas artwork and illustrated smoke remain flat light on real wall/ceiling surfaces. No floating 3D objects. No frozen opening or ending. Use the exact architecture and artwork in this shot's references; animate only illustrated characters actually present, never invent source-template deer or rabbits. Natural room audio only, no music.

ACTION TIMELINE:

1.6–2.4s — The whole tree swings down diagonally across the frame and crashes flat onto the wooden floor, branches flattening, the gold star snapping off the top and skittering away on its own.
2.4–3.2s — She lands on the floor face-down across the fallen tree, and the impact bursts a wave of ornaments off the branches — dozens of red, gold, pink and white baubles bouncing out low across the wooden floor and rolling toward the camera, spreading right across the bottom of the frame.
3.2–5.0s — The last ornaments wobble to a stop on the floor. She lies still across the fallen tree for a beat, then lifts her head, blows the hair out of her face and starts laughing. The two children stay where they moved to on the left, staring at the mess. End frame: the tree flat on the floor, ornaments scattered everywhere, her looking up laughing.

AUDIO: her real voice, lip-synced, matching her appearance — a short yelp as she goes over, then breathless laughing at the end; the dry rush of the tree branches going over; the heavy thud of the tree hitting the wooden floor; dozens of glass and plastic ornaments clattering, bouncing and rolling on hard wood; one small surprised squeal from a child. Real weight and gravity — the tree topples on its own mass and stops on the floor, she drops straight down, nobody is thrown or launched. No subtitles, no captions and no on-screen words of any kind anywhere in the picture.
